This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice.     "- Explore: Read-only search agent for broad fan-out searches — when answering means sweeping many files, directories, or naming conventions and you only need the conclusion, not the file dumps. 1	import { useEffect, useLayoutEffect, useRef } from "react";
2	import type React from "react";
3	import { conversationMainManager } from "@/components/component-state-managers";
4	import { getRunLatestUnreadTimestamp } from "@/lib/conversation-state";
5	import type { ComposerMode } from "./types";
6	import type { AgentSnapshot, ComposerWorkerOption, MessageRecord, RunRecord, WorkerType } from "./types";
7	import { homeUiStateManager } from "./HomeUiStateManager";
8	import { DEFAULT_COMPOSER_EFFORT } from "./constants";
9	import { getWorkerModelOptions, parseWorkerType, resolveComposerEffortLabel, resolveComposerModelValue } from "./utils";
10	import { useRuntimeAPIs } from "@/runtime-api/provider";
11	
12	const CONVERSATION_BOTTOM_THRESHOLD_PX = 8;
13	const CONVERSATION_MEANINGFUL_OVERFLOW_PX = 112;
14	const SCROLL_AREA_VIEWPORT_SELECTOR = '[data-slot="scroll-area-viewport"], [data-radix-scroll-area-viewport]';
15	
16	function textVersion(value: string | null | undefined) {
17	  const text = value ?? "";
18	  if (text.length <= 128) {
19	    return text;
20	  }
21	  return `${text.length}:${text.slice(0, 64)}:${text.slice(-64)}`;
22	}
23	
24	export function shouldConversationFollowLatest(
25	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
26	) {
27	  return metrics.scrollHeight - metrics.clientHeight - metrics.scrollTop <= CONVERSATION_BOTTOM_THRESHOLD_PX;
28	}
29	
30	export function shouldConversationShowOutputBelow(
31	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
32	) {
33	  return metrics.scrollHeight - metrics.clientHeight > CONVERSATION_MEANINGFUL_OVERFLOW_PX
34	    && !shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics);
35	}
36	
37	export function hasMeaningfulConversationOverflow(
38	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
39	) {
40	  return metrics.scrollHeight - metrics.clientHeight > CONVERSATION_MEANINGFUL_OVERFLOW_PX;
41	}
42	
43	/**
44	 * Following the latest output is cancelled by the reader scrolling UP, and by
45	 * nothing else.
46	 *
47	 * This used to end with a bare `shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics)`, which
48	 * meant content growing *beneath* the viewport also cancelled it — the reader
49	 * had not moved, but they were no longer within 8px of the bottom, so the app
50	 * decided they had chosen to stop following. `wasFollowing` keeps the intent
51	 * sticky; a reader who returns to the bottom re-arms it.
52	 */
53	export function shouldConversationKeepFollowingLatest(
54	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">,
55	  previousScrollTop: number,
56	  wasFollowing = false,
57	) {
58	  if (metrics.scrollTop < previousScrollTop) {
59	    return false;
60	  }
61	
62	  return wasFollowing || shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics);
63	}
64	
65	/**
66	 * Re-pin to the bottom when we are supposed to be following but have drifted
67	 * off it — i.e. content grew below the viewport after we positioned.
68	 *
69	 * This replaces a one-shot "retry the initial position" check that was gated on
70	 * `positionedRunId !== selectedRunId` and therefore could fire at most once per
71	 * run, during the very pass that marked the run positioned. If that single
72	 * attempt measured short for any reason, the conversation stayed stranded
73	 * mid-transcript with no way to recover. Making the condition a property of the
74	 * current geometry rather than a one-time flag means it self-corrects for every
75	 * cause of landing short — late layout, async content, or a transcript window
76	 * that grew after an earlier `loadOlder`.
77	 *
78	 * Terminates naturally: once pinned to the bottom
79	 * `shouldConversationFollowLatest` is true and this returns false.
80	 */
81	export function shouldConversationReanchorToLatest({
82	  selectedRunId,
83	  selectedRunHasOutput,
84	  shouldFollowLatest,
85	  metrics,
86	}: {
87	  selectedRunId: string | null;
88	  selectedRunHasOutput: boolean;
89	  shouldFollowLatest: boolean;
90	  metrics: Pick<HTMLDivElement, "scrollTop" | "clientHeight" | "scrollHeight">;
91	}) {
92	  return Boolean(
93	    selectedRunId
94	    && selectedRunHasOutput
95	    && shouldFollowLatest
96	    && hasMeaningfulConversationOverflow(metrics)
97	    && !shouldConversationFollowLatest(metrics),
98	  );
99	}
100	
101	export function getConversationOutputVersion(
102	  selectedRunId: string | null,
103	  messages: MessageRecord[] | undefined,
104	  agents: AgentSnapshot[] | undefined,
105	) {
106	  if (!selectedRunId) {
107	    return "empty";
108	  }
109	
110	  const messageVersion = (messages ?? [])
111	    .filter((message) => message.runId === selectedRunId)
112	    .map((message) => `${message.id}:${message.createdAt}:${textVersion(message.content)}`)
113	    .join("|");
114	  const agentVersion = (agents ?? [])
115	    .map((agent) => {
116	      const outputEntriesVersion = (agent.outputEntries ?? [])
117	        .map((entry) => `${entry.id}:${entry.type}:${entry.status ?? ""}:${textVersion(entry.text)}`)
118	        .join(",");
119	      return `${agent.name}:${textVersion(agent.currentText)}:${textVersion(agent.lastText)}:${outputEntriesVersion}`;
120	    })
121	    .join("|");
122	
123	  return `${selectedRunId}::${messageVersion}::${agentVersion}`;
124	}
125	
126	export function hasSelectedRunMessageOutput(
127	  selectedRunId: string | null,
128	  messages: MessageRecord[] | undefined,
129	) {
130	  if (!selectedRunId) {
131	    return false;
132	  }
133	
134	  return (messages ?? []).some((message) => (
135	    message.runId === selectedRunId && Boolean(message.content.trim())
136	  ));
137	}
138	
139	interface UseRunSelectionEffectsProps {
140	  scrollRef: React.RefObject<HTMLDivElement | null>;
141	  state: { messages?: MessageRecord[]; agents?: AgentSnapshot[] };
142	  selectedRunId: string | null;
143	  selectedRun: RunRecord | null;
144	  activeComposerMode: ComposerMode;
145	  selectedCliAgent: ComposerWorkerOption;
146	  autoSelectedWorkerType: WorkerType | null;
147	  activeAllowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];
148	  setHydratedRunSelectionId: React.Dispatch<React.SetStateAction<string | null>>;
149	  availableWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];
150	  configuredAllowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];
151	  apiKeys: Record<string, string>;
152	  setApiKeys: React.Dispatch<React.SetStateAction<Record<string, string>>>;
153	  readMarkers: Record<string, string>;
154	  setReadMarkers: React.Dispatch<React.SetStateAction<Record<string, string>>>;
155	}
156	
157	export function resolveRunComposerSelection(args: {
158	  run: RunRecord;
159	  activeAllowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];
160	}) {
161	  const preferredWorker = parseWorkerType(args.run.preferredWorkerType);
162	  const worker: ComposerWorkerOption = preferredWorker && args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes.includes(preferredWorker)
163	    ? preferredWorker
164	    : "auto";
165	  const modelWorker = preferredWorker ?? args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes[0] ?? "codex";
166	  return {
167	    conversationMode: args.run.mode === "direct" || args.run.mode === "commit" ? "direct" as const : "omni" as const,
168	    worker,
169	    model: resolveComposerModelValue(args.run.preferredWorkerModel)
170	      ?? getWorkerModelOptions(undefined, modelWorker)[0]?.value
171	      ?? "",
172	    effort: resolveComposerEffortLabel(args.run.preferredWorkerEffort) ?? DEFAULT_COMPOSER_EFFORT,
173	    accountId: args.run.preferredWorkerAccountId?.trim() || "auto",
174	  };
175	}
176	
177	function runSelectionVersion(run: RunRecord) {
178	  return JSON.stringify([
179	    run.preferredWorkerRevision ?? 0,
180	    run.preferredWorkerType ?? null,
181	    run.preferredWorkerModel ?? null,
182	    run.preferredWorkerEffort ?? null,
183	    run.preferredWorkerAccountId ?? null,
184	    run.mode,
185	  ]);
186	}
187	
188	export function useRunSelectionEffects({
189	  scrollRef,
190	  state,
191	  selectedRunId,
192	  selectedRun,
193	  activeComposerMode,
194	  selectedCliAgent,
195	  autoSelectedWorkerType,
196	  activeAllowedWorkerTypes,
197	  setHydratedRunSelectionId,
198	  availableWorkerTypes,
199	  configuredAllowedWorkerTypes,
200	  apiKeys,
201	  setApiKeys,
202	  readMarkers,
203	  setReadMarkers,
204	}: UseRunSelectionEffectsProps) {
205	  const runtimeApis = useRuntimeAPIs();
206	  const shouldFollowLatestRef = useRef(true);
207	  const previousScrollTopRef = useRef(0);
208	  const previousSelectedRunIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
209	  const previousOutputVersionRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
210	  const instantPositionedRunIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null);
211	  const persistedReadMarkerRef = useRef<Map<string, string>>(new Map());
212	  const outputVersion = getConversationOutputVersion(selectedRunId, state.messages, state.agents);
213	  const selectedRunHasOutput = hasSelectedRunMessageOutput(selectedRunId, state.messages);
214	
215	  useEffect(() => {
216	    const viewport = scrollRef.current?.querySelector(SCROLL_AREA_VIEWPORT_SELECTOR) as HTMLDivElement | null;
217	    if (!viewport) {
218	      conversationMainManager.setHasOutputBelow(false);
219	      return;
220	    }
221	
222	    const positionAtLatest = (behavior: ScrollBehavior) => {
223	      viewport.scrollTo({
224	        top: viewport.scrollHeight,
225	        behavior,
226	      });
227	      previousScrollTopRef.current = viewport.scrollTop;
228	      shouldFollowLatestRef.current = true;
229	      instantPositionedRunIdRef.current = selectedRunId;
230	    };
231	
232	    const updateOutputBelowState = () => {
233	      if (shouldConversationReanchorToLatest({
234	        selectedRunId,
235	        selectedRunHasOutput,
236	        shouldFollowLatest: shouldFollowLatestRef.current,
237	        metrics: viewport,
238	      })) {
239	        positionAtLatest("auto");
240	      }
241	      conversationMainManager.setHasOutputBelow(shouldConversationShowOutputBelow(viewport));
242	    };
243	
244	    previousScrollTopRef.current = viewport.scrollTop;
245	
246	    const updateFollowState = () => {
247	      shouldFollowLatestRef.current = shouldConversationKeepFollowingLatest(
248	        viewport,
249	        previousScrollTopRef.current,
250	        shouldFollowLatestRef.current,
251	      );
252	      previousScrollTopRef.current = viewport.scrollTop;
253	      updateOutputBelowState();
254	    };
255	
256	    updateFollowState();
257	    viewport.addEventListener("scroll", updateFollowState, { passive: true });
258	
259	    const resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver(updateOutputBelowState);
260	    resizeObserver.observe(viewport);
261	    if (viewport.firstElementChild) {
262	      resizeObserver.observe(viewport.firstElementChild);
263	    }
264	
265	    return () => {
266	      viewport.removeEventListener("scroll", updateFollowState);
267	      resizeObserver.disconnect();
268	      conversationMainManager.setHasOutputBelow(false);
269	    };
270	  }, [scrollRef, selectedRunHasOutput, selectedRunId]);
271	
272	  useLayoutEffect(() => {
273	    const viewport = scrollRef.current?.querySelector(SCROLL_AREA_VIEWPORT_SELECTOR) as HTMLDivElement | null;
274	    if (!viewport) {
275	      return;
276	    }
277	
278	    const runChanged = previousSelectedRunIdRef.current !== selectedRunId;
279	    if (runChanged) {
280	      instantPositionedRunIdRef.current = null;
281	    }
282	    previousSelectedRunIdRef.current = selectedRunId;
283	    const outputChanged = previousOutputVersionRef.current !== outputVersion;
284	    previousOutputVersionRef.current = outputVersion;
285	
286	    if (!runChanged && !outputChanged) {
287	      return;
288	    }
289	
290	    if (!runChanged && !shouldFollowLatestRef.current) {
291	      return;
292	    }
293	
294	    const shouldRestoreInstantly = runChanged
295	      || (selectedRunHasOutput && instantPositionedRunIdRef.current !== selectedRunId);
296	    const scrollBehavior: ScrollBehavior = shouldRestoreInstantly ? "auto" : "smooth";
297	
298	    const hasOverflow = hasMeaningfulConversationOverflow(viewport);
299	    viewport.scrollTo({
300	      top: viewport.scrollHeight,
301	      behavior: scrollBehavior,
302	    });
303	    previousScrollTopRef.current = viewport.scrollTop;
304	    if (selectedRunHasOutput && hasOverflow) {
305	      instantPositionedRunIdRef.current = selectedRunId;
306	    }
307	    shouldFollowLatestRef.current = true;
308	  }, [scrollRef, outputVersion, selectedRunHasOutput, selectedRunId]);
309	
310	  useEffect(() => {
311	    if (!selectedRunId || !selectedRun) {
312	      setHydratedRunSelectionId(null);
313	      if (activeComposerMode === "direct") {
314	        const nextDirectWorker = selectedCliAgent === "auto" ? (autoSelectedWorkerType ?? activeAllowedWorkerTypes[0] ?? "codex") : selectedCliAgent;
315	        if (!activeAllowedWorkerTypes.includes(nextDirectWorker as WorkerType)) {
316	          homeUiStateManager.setComposerSelectionField("worker", autoSelectedWorkerType ?? activeAllowedWorkerTypes[0] ?? "codex", { userEdited: false });
317	        } else if (nextDirectWorker !== selectedCliAgent) {
318	          homeUiStateManager.setComposerSelectionField("worker", nextDirectWorker, { userEdited: false });
319	        }
320	      } else if (selectedCliAgent !== "auto" && !activeAllowedWorkerTypes.includes(selectedCliAgent)) {
321	        homeUiStateManager.setComposerSelectionField("worker", "auto", { userEdited: false });
322	      }
323	      return;
324	    }
325	
326	    const runSelection = resolveRunComposerSelection({
327	      run: selectedRun,
328	      activeAllowedWorkerTypes,
329	    });
330	    homeUiStateManager.hydrateComposerSelection({
331	      runId: selectedRunId,
332	      selection: runSelection,
333	      serverVersion: runSelectionVersion(selectedRun),
334	    });
335	  }, [
336	    activeComposerMode,
337	    activeAllowedWorkerTypes,
338	    autoSelectedWorkerType,
339	    selectedRun,
340	    selectedRunId,
341	  ]);
342	
343	  useEffect(() => {
344	    if (availableWorkerTypes.length === 0) {
345	      return;
346	    }
347	
348	    const availableSet = new Set(availableWorkerTypes);
349	    const sanitizedAllowed = configuredAllowedWorkerTypes.filter((type) => availableSet.has(type));
350	    const nextAllowed = sanitizedAllowed.length > 0 ? sanitizedAllowed : [...availableWorkerTypes];
351	    const normalizedDefault = nextAllowed.includes(apiKeys.WORKER_DEFAULT_TYPE as WorkerType)
352	      ? apiKeys.WORKER_DEFAULT_TYPE
353	      : nextAllowed[0];
354	
355	    if (
356	      JSON.stringify(nextAllowed) === JSON.stringify(configuredAllowedWorkerTypes) &&
357	      normalizedDefault === apiKeys.WORKER_DEFAULT_TYPE
358	    ) {
359	      return;
360	    }
361	
362	    setApiKeys((current) => ({
363	      ...current,
364	      WORKER_ALLOWED_TYPES: JSON.stringify(nextAllowed),
365	      WORKER_DEFAULT_TYPE: normalizedDefault,
366	    }));
367	  }, [apiKeys.WORKER_DEFAULT_TYPE, availableWorkerTypes, configuredAllowedWorkerTypes, setApiKeys]);
368	
369	  useEffect(() => {
370	    if (!selectedRunId) {
371	      return;
372	    }
373	
374	    if (!selectedRun) {
375	      return;
376	    }
377	
378	    const latestForSelected = getRunLatestUnreadTimestamp(selectedRun, state.messages || []);
379	    if (!latestForSelected) {
380	      return;
381	    }
382	
383	    if (readMarkers[selectedRunId] === latestForSelected) {
384	      return;
385	    }
386	
387	    setReadMarkers((current) => {
388	      if (current[selectedRunId] === latestForSelected) {
389	        return current;
390	      }
391	      return { ...current, [selectedRunId]: latestForSelected };
392	    });
393	
394	    const persistedKey = `${selectedRunId}:${latestForSelected}`;
395	    if (persistedReadMarkerRef.current.get(selectedRunId) === persistedKey) {
396	      return;
397	    }
398	    persistedReadMarkerRef.current.set(selectedRunId, persistedKey);
399	
400	    void runtimeApis.runs.act({
401	      runId: selectedRunId,
402	      body: { action: "mark_read" },
403	    }).catch((error) => {
404	      persistedReadMarkerRef.current.delete(selectedRunId);
405	      console.warn("Failed to persist conversation read marker:", error);
406	    });
407	  }, [readMarkers, runtimeApis.runs, selectedRunId, selectedRun, state.messages, setReadMarkers]);
408	}

190	export class HomeUiStateManager extends StateManager<HomeUiState> {
191	  private readonly acknowledgedSelectionsByRun = new Map<string, ComposerSelection>();
192	
193	  constructor() {
194	    super(initialHomeUiState);
195	  }
196	
197	  private revokeAttachmentPreview(attachment: PendingChatAttachment) {
198	    if (attachment.previewUrl && typeof URL !== "undefined" && typeof URL.revokeObjectURL === "function") {
199	      URL.revokeObjectURL(attachment.previewUrl);
200	    }
201	  }
202	
203	  addAttachmentFiles(files: File[]) {
204	    if (files.length === 0) {
205	      return;
206	    }
207	
208	    this.setKey("attachments", (current) => [
209	      ...current,
210	      ...files.map((file) => {
211	        const mimeType = file.type || "application/octet-stream";
212	        const kind = chatAttachmentKindFromMimeType(mimeType);
213	        const previewUrl = kind === "image" && typeof URL !== "undefined" && typeof URL.createObjectURL === "function"
214	          ? URL.createObjectURL(file)
215	          : undefined;
216	
217	        const randomId = typeof crypto !== "undefined" && typeof crypto.randomUUID === "function"
218	          ? crypto.randomUUID()
219	          : Math.random().toString(36).slice(2);
220	
221	        return {
222	          id: `${Date.now()}-${randomId}`,
223	          kind,
224	          name: file.name || "attachment",
225	          mimeType,
226	          size: file.size,
227	          file,
228	          ...(previewUrl ? { previewUrl } : {}),
229	        };
230	      }),
231	    ]);
232	  }
233	
234	  addPastedImages(files: File[]) {
235	    this.addAttachmentFiles(files.map((file, index) => {
236	      if (file.name) {
237	        return file;
238	      }
239	
240	      const extension = file.type.split("/")[1]?.split(";")[0] || "png";
241	      return new File([file], `pasted-image-${Date.now()}-${index}.${extension}`, {
242	        type: file.type || "image/png",
243	        lastModified: file.lastModified || Date.now(),
244	      });
245	    }));
246	  }
247	
248	  removeAttachment(id: string) {
249	    this.setKey("attachments", (current) => {
250	      const removed = current.find((attachment) => attachment.id === id);
251	      if (removed) {
252	        this.revokeAttachmentPreview(removed);
253	      }
254	      return current.filter((attachment) => attachment.id !== id);
255	    });
256	  }
257	
258	  clearAttachments() {
259	    const current = this.getSnapshot().attachments;
260	    current.forEach((attachment) => this.revokeAttachmentPreview(attachment));
261	    this.setKey("attachments", []);
262	  }
263	
264	  setComposerDraft(patch: Partial<ComposerDraft>) {
265	    this.update((current) => {
266	      const command = patch.command ?? current.command;
267	      const commandCursor = patch.commandCursor ?? current.commandCursor;
268	      const mentionIndex = patch.mentionIndex ?? current.mentionIndex;
269	      const attachments = patch.attachments ?? current.attachments;
270	
271	      if (
272	        Object.is(command, current.command)
273	        && Object.is(commandCursor, current.commandCursor)
274	        && Object.is(mentionIndex, current.mentionIndex)
275	        && Object.is(attachments, current.attachments)
276	      ) {
277	        return current;
278	      }
279	
280	      return {
281	        ...current,
282	        command,
283	        commandCursor,
284	        mentionIndex,
285	        attachments,
286	      };
287	    });
288	  }
289	
290	  private activeSelection(state: HomeUiState): ComposerSelection {
291	    return {
292	      conversationMode: state.selectedConversationMode,
293	      worker: state.selectedCliAgent,
294	      accountId: state.selectedWorkerAccountId,
295	      model: state.selectedModel,
296	      effort: state.selectedEffort,
297	    };
298	  }
299	
300	  setComposerSelectionField<TKey extends ComposerSelectionField>(
301	    field: TKey,
302	    value: StateUpdate<ComposerSelection[TKey]>,
303	    options: { userEdited?: boolean } = {},
304	  ) {
305	    this.update((current) => {
306	      const currentSelection = this.activeSelection(current);
307	      const nextValue = typeof value === "function"
308	        ? (value as (previous: ComposerSelection[TKey]) => ComposerSelection[TKey])(currentSelection[field])
309	        : value;
310	      if (Object.is(currentSelection[field], nextValue)) return current;
311	
312	      const key = current.selectedRunId ?? NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY;
313	      const stored = current.composerDraftsByRun[key];
314	      const dirtySelectionFields = new Set(stored?.dirtySelectionFields ?? []);
315	      if (options.userEdited !== false) {
316	        const acknowledgedValue = current.selectedRunId
317	          ? this.acknowledgedSelectionsByRun.get(current.selectedRunId)?.[field]
318	          : undefined;
319	        if (current.selectedRunId && Object.is(nextValue, acknowledgedValue)) {
320	          dirtySelectionFields.delete(field);
321	        } else {
322	          dirtySelectionFields.add(field);
323	        }
324	      }
325	      const selection = { ...(stored?.selection ?? currentSelection), [field]: nextValue };
326	      const draft: ComposerDraft = {
327	        command: current.command,
328	        commandCursor: current.commandCursor,
329	        mentionIndex: current.mentionIndex,
330	        attachments: current.attachments,
331	        selection,
332	        dirtySelectionFields: [...dirtySelectionFields],
333	        serverSelectionVersion: stored?.serverSelectionVersion ?? null,
334	      };
335	
336	      const selectionStatePatch: Partial<HomeUiState> = field === "conversationMode"
337	        ? { selectedConversationMode: nextValue as ConversationModeOption }
338	        : field === "worker"
339	          ? { selectedCliAgent: nextValue as ComposerWorkerOption }
340	          : field === "accountId"
341	            ? { selectedWorkerAccountId: nextValue as string }
342	            : field === "model"
343	              ? { selectedModel: nextValue as string }
344	              : { selectedEffort: nextValue as string };
345	
346	      return {
347	        ...current,
348	        ...selectionStatePatch,
349	        composerDraftsByRun: { ...current.composerDraftsByRun, [key]: draft },
350	      };
351	    });
352	  }
353	
354	  setComposerWorkerSelection(worker: ComposerWorkerOption, model: string) {
355	    this.update((current) => {
356	      const currentSelection = this.activeSelection(current);
357	      const workerChanged = currentSelection.worker !== worker;
358	      const modelChanged = currentSelection.model !== model;
359	      if (!workerChanged && !modelChanged) return current;
360	
361	      const key = current.selectedRunId ?? NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY;
362	      const stored = current.composerDraftsByRun[key];
363	      const dirtySelectionFields = new Set(stored?.dirtySelectionFields ?? []);
364	      const acknowledged = current.selectedRunId
365	        ? this.acknowledgedSelectionsByRun.get(current.selectedRunId)
366	        : undefined;
367	
368	      if (workerChanged) {
369	        if (current.selectedRunId && acknowledged?.worker === worker) {
370	          dirtySelectionFields.delete("worker");
371	        } else {
372	          dirtySelectionFields.add("worker");
373	        }
374	      }
375	      if (modelChanged) {
376	        if (current.selectedRunId && acknowledged?.model === model) {
377	          dirtySelectionFields.delete("model");
378	        } else {
379	          dirtySelectionFields.add("model");
380	        }
381	      }
382	
383	      const selection = { ...currentSelection, worker, model };
384	      const draft: ComposerDraft = {
385	        command: current.command,
386	        commandCursor: current.commandCursor,
387	        mentionIndex: current.mentionIndex,
388	        attachments: current.attachments,
389	        selection,
390	        dirtySelectionFields: [...dirtySelectionFields],
391	        serverSelectionVersion: stored?.serverSelectionVersion ?? null,
392	      };
393	
394	      return {
395	        ...current,
396	        selectedCliAgent: worker,
397	        selectedModel: model,
398	        composerDraftsByRun: { ...current.composerDraftsByRun, [key]: draft },
399	      };
400	    });
401	  }
402	
403	  hydrateComposerSelection(args: {
404	    runId: string;
405	    selection: ComposerSelection;
406	    serverVersion: string;
407	  }) {
408	    this.acknowledgedSelectionsByRun.set(args.runId, { ...args.selection });
409	    this.update((current) => {
410	      const key = args.runId;
411	      const active = current.selectedRunId === args.runId;
412	      const currentSelection = active
413	        ? this.activeSelection(current)
414	        : current.composerDraftsByRun[key]?.selection ?? args.selection;
415	      const stored = current.composerDraftsByRun[key];
416	      if (
417	        stored?.serverSelectionVersion === args.serverVersion
418	        && stored.dirtySelectionFields.length === 0
419	      ) return current;
420	
421	      const dirtyFields = new Set(stored?.dirtySelectionFields ?? []);
422	      const nextSelection = { ...currentSelection };
423	      for (const field of Object.keys(args.selection) as ComposerSelectionField[]) {
424	        if (dirtyFields.has(field)) {
425	          if (Object.is(currentSelection[field], args.selection[field])) dirtyFields.delete(field);
426	          continue;
427	        }
428	        (nextSelection[field] as ComposerSelection[typeof field]) = args.selection[field];
429	      }
430	
431	      const draft: ComposerDraft = {
432	        command: active ? current.command : stored?.command ?? "",
433	        commandCursor: active ? current.commandCursor : stored?.commandCursor ?? 0,
434	        mentionIndex: active ? current.mentionIndex : stored?.mentionIndex ?? 0,
435	        attachments: active ? current.attachments : stored?.attachments ?? [],
436	        selection: nextSelection,
437	        dirtySelectionFields: [...dirtyFields],
438	        serverSelectionVersion: args.serverVersion,
439	      };
440	
441	      return {
442	        ...current,
443	        ...(active ? {
444	          selectedConversationMode: nextSelection.conversationMode,
445	          selectedCliAgent: nextSelection.worker,
446	          selectedWorkerAccountId: nextSelection.accountId,
447	          selectedModel: nextSelection.model,
448	          selectedEffort: nextSelection.effort,
449	          hydratedRunSelectionId: args.runId,
450	        } : {}),
451	        composerDraftsByRun: { ...current.composerDraftsByRun, [key]: draft },
452	      };
453	    });
454	  }
455	
456	  selectRun(nextRunId: string | null) {
457	    this.update((current) => {
458	      if (current.selectedRunId === nextRunId) return current;
459	      const prevKey = current.selectedRunId ?? NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY;
460	      const nextKey = nextRunId ?? NEW_CONVERSATION_DRAFT_KEY;
461	      const nextDraft = current.composerDraftsByRun[nextKey] ?? EMPTY_COMPOSER_DRAFT;
462	      const drafts: Record<string, ComposerDraft> = { ...current.composerDraftsByRun };
463	      const hasContent = current.command.length > 0
464	        || current.commandCursor !== 0
465	        || current.mentionIndex !== 0
466	        || current.attachments.length > 0
467	        || (current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]?.dirtySelectionFields.length ?? 0) > 0;
468	      // A clean, server-hydrated selection is still the session's compound
469	      // composer state. Keep it in memory so switching back restores the
470	      // model/effort/worker atomically instead of flashing the generic new-run
471	      // defaults until the hydration effect runs again.
472	      if (hasContent || current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]) {
473	        drafts[prevKey] = {
474	          command: current.command,
475	          commandCursor: current.commandCursor,
476	          mentionIndex: current.mentionIndex,
477	          attachments: current.attachments,
478	          selection: this.activeSelection(current),
479	          dirtySelectionFields: current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]?.dirtySelectionFields ?? [],
480	          serverSelectionVersion: current.composerDraftsByRun[prevKey]?.serverSelectionVersion ?? null,
481	        };
482	      } else {
483	        delete drafts[prevKey];
484	      }
485	      if (nextDraft === EMPTY_COMPOSER_DRAFT) {
486	        delete drafts[nextKey];
487	      }
488	      return {
489	        ...current,
490	        selectedRunId: nextRunId,
491	        composerDraftsByRun: drafts,
492	        command: nextDraft.command,
493	        commandCursor: nextDraft.commandCursor,
494	        mentionIndex: nextDraft.mentionIndex,
495	        attachments: nextDraft.attachments,
496	        selectedConversationMode: nextDraft.selection.conversationMode,
497	        selectedCliAgent: nextDraft.selection.worker,
498	        selectedWorkerAccountId: nextDraft.selection.accountId,
499	        selectedModel: nextDraft.selection.model,
500	        selectedEffort: nextDraft.selection.effort,
501	        hydratedRunSelectionId: nextRunId && nextDraft.serverSelectionVersion ? nextRunId : null,
502	      };
503	    });
504	  }
505	
506	  setRenamingRunId(runId: string | null) {
507	    this.patch((current) => current.renamingRunId === runId
508	      ? {}
509	      : { renamingRunId: runId, renameDialogRevision: current.renameDialogRevision + 1 });
510	  }

513	    this.patch((current) => current.movingRunId === runId
514	      ? {}
515	      : { movingRunId: runId, moveDialogRevision: current.moveDialogRevision + 1 });
516	  }

518	  revealMoreProjectSessions(projectPath: string) {
519	    this.setKey("visibleProjectSessionCounts", (current) => ({
520	      ...current,
521	      [projectPath]: (current[projectPath] ?? PROJECT_SESSION_DISPLAY_BATCH_SIZE) + PROJECT_SESSION_DISPLAY_BATCH_SIZE,
522	    }));
523	  }
524	
525	  resetProjectSessionDisplayLimit(projectPath: string) {
526	    this.setKey("visibleProjectSessionCounts", (current) => {
527	      if (!(projectPath in current)) {
528	        return current;
529	      }
530	
531	      const next = { ...current };
532	      delete next[projectPath];
533	      return next;
534	    });
535	  }
536	
537	  setProjectExpanded(projectPath: string, expanded: boolean) {
538	    this.setKey("collapsedProjectPaths", (current) => {
539	      const next = new Set(current);
540	      if (expanded) {
541	        if (!next.has(projectPath)) return current;
542	        next.delete(projectPath);
543	      } else {
544	        if (next.has(projectPath)) return current;
545	        next.add(projectPath);
546	      }
547	      return next;
548	    });
549	  }
550	
551	  collapseProjects(projectPaths: string[]) {
552	    if (projectPaths.length === 0) return;
553	
554	    this.update((current) => {
555	      const collapsedProjectPaths = new Set(current.collapsedProjectPaths);
556	      let changed = false;
557	      for (const projectPath of projectPaths) {
558	        if (!collapsedProjectPaths.has(projectPath)) {
559	          collapsedProjectPaths.add(projectPath);
560	          changed = true;
561	        }
562	      }
563	
564	      if (!changed) return current;
565	
566	      const visibleProjectSessionCounts = { ...current.visibleProjectSessionCounts };
567	      for (const projectPath of projectPaths) {
568	        delete visibleProjectSessionCounts[projectPath];
569	      }
570	
571	      return {
572	        ...current,
573	        collapsedProjectPaths,
574	        visibleProjectSessionCounts,
575	      };
576	    });
577	  }
578	
579	  createSetter<TKey extends keyof HomeUiState>(key: TKey) {
580	    return (value: StateUpdate<HomeUiState[TKey]>) => {
581	      this.setKey(key, value);
582	    };
583	  }
584	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:42:40.179Z

The worker record shows opus launched right at run creation, matching that timestamp — so it seems the model was already opus-5 from the very first message, even though the user recalls selecting Fable 5.1 and seeing it switch later. I'm wondering if `effective_launch_model` got overwritten during a relaunch, since turn_generation being 2 suggests multiple turns happened on the same worker.

The run's preferred_worker_model is claude-opus-5 at revision 0, meaning it was set right at creation — so the client's creation request itself already specified opus, not fable. Maybe the server normalized or rejected "claude-fable-5-1" and fell back to a default model. I should check how the server resolves the model on run creation, so I'm going to look at worker-models.ts.

82	async function defaultRunCommand(command: string, args: string[], env: EnvLike = process.env) {
83	  const managedEnv = withManagedPath(env, undefined, { loginShellPathMode: "cached" });
84	  const executable = command === "codex"
85	    ? resolveCodexCommand({ env: managedEnv })
86	    : resolveCommand(command, { env: managedEnv });
87	  if (!executable) {
88	    throw new Error(`Unable to resolve ${command} from the managed agent environment.`);
89	  }
90	
91	  const result = await execFileAsync(executable, args, {
92	    env: managedEnv as NodeJS.ProcessEnv,
93	    timeout: 5_000,
94	    maxBuffer: 8 * 1024 * 1024,
95	  });
96	
97	  return result.stdout;
98	}
99	
100	function mergeModelOptions(base: WorkerModelOption[], discovered: WorkerModelOption[]) {
101	  const seen = new Set<string>();
102	  const merged: WorkerModelOption[] = [];
103	
104	  for (const model of [...base, ...discovered]) {
105	    const value = model.value.trim();
106	    if (!value || seen.has(value)) {
107	      continue;
108	    }
109	
110	    seen.add(value);
111	    merged.push({
112	      value,
113	      label: normalizeLabel(value, model.label),
114	      ...(model.source ? { source: model.source } : {}),
115	    });
116	  }
117	
118	  return merged;
119	}
120	
121	function buildHardcodedCatalog(): WorkerModelCatalog {
122	  return {
123	    codex: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.codex],
124	    claude: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.claude],
125	    gemini: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.gemini],
126	    opencode: [...HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.opencode],
127	  };
128	}
129	
130	export function getBuiltInWorkerModelOptions(workerType: SupportedWorkerType): WorkerModelOption[] {
131	  return [...buildHardcodedCatalog()[workerType]];
132	}
133	
134	export function mergeClaudeGatewayModelsIntoCatalog(
135	  catalog: WorkerModelCatalog,
136	  input: { custom?: ClaudeGatewayModelInput[]; discovered?: ClaudeGatewayModelInput[] },
137	): WorkerModelCatalog {
138	  const gatewayModels = mergeClaudeGatewayModels(input).map((model) => ({
139	    value: model.value,
140	    label: model.label,
141	    source: "gateway" as const,
142	  }));
143	  return {
144	    ...catalog,
145	    codex: [...catalog.codex],
146	    claude: mergeModelOptions(catalog.claude, gatewayModels),
147	    gemini: [...catalog.gemini],
148	    opencode: [...catalog.opencode],
149	  };
150	}
151	
152	function normalizeCachedCatalog(catalog: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null | undefined): Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null {
153	  if (!catalog || typeof catalog !== "object") {
154	    return null;
155	  }
156	
157	  const normalized: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> = {};
158	  for (const type of Object.keys(HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS) as SupportedWorkerType[]) {
159	    const models = catalog[type];
160	    if (!Array.isArray(models)) {
161	      continue;
162	    }
163	
164	    const options = models.flatMap((model) => {
165	      if (!model || typeof model !== "object") {
166	        return [];
167	      }
168	
169	      const value = typeof model.value === "string" ? model.value.trim() : "";
170	      const isIncompatibleCodexModel = type === "codex"
171	        && (value.startsWith("claude-") || value.startsWith("anthropic/"));
172	      if (!value || isIncompatibleCodexModel || DEPRECATED_WORKER_MODELS[type]?.has(value)) {
173	        return [];
174	      }
175	
176	      const label = typeof model.label === "string" ? model.label : undefined;
177	      return [{
178	        value,
179	        label: normalizeLabel(value, label),
180	      }];
181	    });
182	
183	    if (options.length > 0) {
184	      normalized[type] = mergeModelOptions([], options);
185	    }
186	  }
187	
188	  return Object.keys(normalized).length > 0 ? normalized : null;
189	}
190	
191	function mergeCatalogWithCache(cachedCatalog: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null | undefined): WorkerModelCatalog {
192	  const catalog = buildHardcodedCatalog();
193	  const normalizedCache = normalizeCachedCatalog(cachedCatalog);
194	
195	  if (!normalizedCache) {
196	    return catalog;
197	  }
198	
199	  for (const type of Object.keys(HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS) as SupportedWorkerType[]) {
200	    catalog[type] = mergeModelOptions(catalog[type], normalizedCache[type] ?? []);
201	  }
202	
203	  return catalog;
204	}
205	
206	function parseCodexModels(output: string) {
207	  const parsed = JSON.parse(output) as {
208	    models?: Array<{
209	      slug?: unknown;
210	      display_name?: unknown;
211	      visibility?: unknown;
212	      priority?: unknown;
213	    }>;
214	  };
215	
216	  return (parsed.models ?? [])
217	    .filter((model) => model.visibility !== "hidden" && model.visibility !== "hide")
218	    .map((model) => {
219	      const value = typeof model.slug === "string" ? model.slug.trim() : "";
220	      if (!value) {
221	        return null;
222	      }
223	
224	      return {
225	        value,
226	        label: normalizeLabel(value, typeof model.display_name === "string" ? model.display_name : undefined),
227	        priority: typeof model.priority === "number" ? model.priority : Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER,
228	      };
229	    })
230	    .filter((model): model is WorkerModelOption & { priority: number } => model !== null)
231	    .sort((left, right) => left.priority - right.priority);
232	}
233	
234	function parseOpenCodeModels(output: string) {
235	  const modelIds = new Set<string>();
236	  const modelPattern = /\b[a-z][a-z0-9_-]*\/[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._:-]*(?:-[a-z0-9._:-]+)*\b/gi;
237	
238	  for (const match of output.matchAll(modelPattern)) {
239	    modelIds.add(match[0]);
240	  }
241	
242	  return [...modelIds].map((value) => ({
243	    value,
244	    label: labelFromModelId(value),
245	  }));
246	}
247	
248	export class WorkerModelCatalogManager {
249	  private readonly runCommand: RunCommand;
250	  private readonly env: EnvLike;
251	  private readonly loadCachedCatalog?: LoadCachedCatalog;
252	  private readonly saveCachedCatalog?: SaveCachedCatalog;
253	  private cachedCatalog: Partial<WorkerModelCatalog> | null | undefined;
254	  private refreshPromise: Promise<WorkerModelCatalog> | null = null;
255	  private hasStartedRefresh = false;
256	
257	  constructor(options: WorkerModelCatalogManagerOptions = {}) {
258	    this.env = options.env ?? process.env;
259	    this.runCommand = options.runCommand ?? ((command, args) => defaultRunCommand(command, args, this.env));
260	    this.loadCachedCatalog = options.loadCachedCatalog;
261	    this.saveCachedCatalog = options.saveCachedCatalog;
262	  }
263	
264	  async getCatalogSnapshot(options: { refreshOnFirstLoad?: boolean } = {}): Promise<WorkerModelCatalogSnapshot> {
265	    const cachedCatalog = await this.loadCacheOnce();
266	
267	    if (options.refreshOnFirstLoad && !this.hasStartedRefresh) {
268	      void this.refreshCatalog().catch(() => undefined);
269	    }
270	
271	    return {
272	      catalog: mergeCatalogWithCache(cachedCatalog),
273	      refreshing: this.refreshPromise !== null,
274	    };
275	  }
276	
277	  async refreshCatalog(): Promise<WorkerModelCatalog> {
278	    if (this.refreshPromise) {
279	      return this.refreshPromise;
280	    }
281	
282	    this.hasStartedRefresh = true;
283	    this.refreshPromise = this.buildFreshCatalog()
284	      .then(async (catalog) => {
285	        this.cachedCatalog = catalog;
286	        await this.saveCachedCatalog?.(catalog);
287	        return catalog;
288	      })
289	      .finally(() => {
290	        this.refreshPromise = null;
291	      });
292	
293	    return this.refreshPromise;
294	  }
295	
296	  private async loadCacheOnce() {
297	    if (this.cachedCatalog !== undefined) {
298	      return this.cachedCatalog;
299	    }
300	
301	    try {
302	      this.cachedCatalog = normalizeCachedCatalog(await this.loadCachedCatalog?.() ?? null);
303	    } catch {
304	      this.cachedCatalog = null;
305	    }
306	
307	    return this.cachedCatalog;
308	  }
309	
310	  private async buildFreshCatalog() {
311	    const baseCatalog = mergeCatalogWithCache(await this.loadCacheOnce());
312	    const [codexResult, openCodeResult] = await Promise.allSettled([
313	      this.runCommand("codex", ["debug", "models"]).then(parseCodexModels),
314	      this.runCommand("opencode", ["models", "--refresh"]).then(parseOpenCodeModels),
315	    ]);
316	
317	    if (codexResult.status === "fulfilled") {
318	      baseCatalog.codex = mergeModelOptions(
319	        codexResult.value.filter((model) => !model.value.startsWith("claude-") && !model.value.startsWith("anthropic/")),
320	        HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.codex,
321	      );
322	    }
323	
324	    if (openCodeResult.status === "fulfilled") {
325	      baseCatalog.opencode = mergeModelOptions(HARDCODED_WORKER_MODELS.opencode, openCodeResult.value);
326	    }
327	
328	    return baseCatalog;
329	  }
330	}
331	
332	export async function buildWorkerModelCatalog(options: WorkerModelCatalogManagerOptions = {}): Promise<WorkerModelCatalog> {
333	  return new WorkerModelCatalogManager(options).refreshCatalog();
334	}
335

I should check whether the client sent opus-5 initially or if the server assigned a default, and look for an update path that writes the model without incrementing the revision — I'll grep the server side for the run creation and update logic.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:43:04.088Z

I'm tracing the actual reported bug sequence: a user opens a new composer, selects claude with the Fable 5.1 model, sends the first message which creates a run with that preferred worker model, but by the second message the displayed model appears to have changed unexpectedly.

Now I'm questioning why the database shows the run's preferred_worker_model as opus instead — either the creation logic actually sent opus, or something downstream overwrote it. I want to check whether the preferred_worker_revision gets bumped during an update, so I'm going to look at the relevant update logic in send-message.ts around lines 1417-1439.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:43:11.543Z

If revision 0's preference update never ran, then the model shown at creation was already opus-5 — but that contradicts the user's account of selecting Fable 5.1 initially and having it work for the first message before switching to Opus 5 on the second.

Maybe the reversion happened right at creation, before the first send even completed, or the commitWorkerSettings override in create.ts is taking precedence over the preferred model in commit mode. I want to check the create.ts code around lines 800-960 to see how that override logic works.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:43:19.713Z

The server just honors whatever model the client sent at creation, so the composer was already on Opus 5 from the start even though the user recalls selecting fable 5.1 — likely a misremembered turn. The reproducible bug is that the composer's model selection keeps reverting to Opus 5, the catalog's first entry, instead of persisting whatever was actually used.

I'm tracing the new-conversation draft flow: when there's no run selected, picking a model stores it in the draft under a special "__new__" key, but once the message is sent and a real run id gets created, `selectRun` looks up the draft by that new run id instead of the "__new__" key — falling back to an empty default draft whose model differs, before hydration overwrites it from the run's preferred model. So it seems the "__new__" draft never gets transferred to the newly created run id; I want to check the mutation/create flow to see if there's supposed to be a snapshot transfer step I'm missing.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:44:03.117Z

I'm tracing what happens in `runCommand.onMutate` when selecting the new run — since it has no draft yet, it falls back to an empty composer draft with default model, worker, effort, and mode. That seems problematic, since it would wipe out the composer state right after creation.

Actually, onMutate fires before mutationFn, but mutationFn still reads `selectedModel` from the closure at the time mutate() was invoked, so creation should send the correct model. The issue is what happens after — the optimistic snapshot built for the new run includes worker type and account, but not the model or effort, so those fields never get properly hydrated after the reset.

That gap causes resolution to fall through to the fallback resolver, which picks the first model in the catalog for that worker — Opus 5 — explaining the unwanted flip right after starting a new conversation. I'm now checking whether a later hydration from the real server run (which does have the correct model) would self-correct this, or whether something in the dirty-field tracking prevents that correction from happening.

I'm wondering whether React's batching creates a timing gap — since mutationFn runs after an await in onMutate, there could be a microtask boundary where React flushes the setSelectedRunId state update before mutationFn actually reads selectedModel, which could explain the mismatch.

That timing matches the database exactly: the run gets created with claude-opus-5, revision stuck at 0, and the worker launches opus. It fits the report of fable being selected while typing but opus showing after send. Now I want to verify whether `selectRun` actually clears the selection by checking what `nextDraft` resolves to when looking up the composer draft for the next run key.

The `__new__` draft itself stays intact since command gets cleared before the run id switches, but nothing actually copies that draft over to the new run's key. So the fix has to explicitly carry the composer selection from the `__new__` draft into the new run's draft when a conversation is created, marking those fields appropriately so the hydration logic doesn't overwrite them with the optimistic run snapshot's defaults.

I also need to reconsider `resolveRunComposerSelection`'s fallback to the first catalog entry when a run lacks a model -- that's effectively what's causing the reversion. Given the requirement to always reuse the model from the previous message in the conversation, I need a more deliberate mechanism than just seeding from the current selection whenever a run lacks a draft, since that would break hydration when switching to older conversations that should keep their own history.

The display default might override a legitimate user selection, since dirty-field tracking under the `__new__` key gets orphaned once the run is created.

For the mutation race, I'm weighing whether to capture the selection snapshot at mutate-time inside `onMutate` versus passing it through the variables/payload the caller sends to `runCommand.mutate(...)` — I should trace back to the actual caller before deciding this is even a real race worth engineering around.

I want to check whether there's existing test coverage for composer selection persistence, starting with the home-view-model tests that were modified, and I should also look at `buildOptimisticCreatedConversationSnapshot` to understand what state gets set at creation time.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:45:06.111Z

I'm tracing the timing issue: mutationFn is read lazily when the retryer executes it, which happens after the awaited onMutate call, and since options.mutationFn gets updated on every render via setOptions, a re-render between those two points could cause a stale or reset mutationFn to be used instead. I'm now wondering whether React flushes state updates synchronously in this async context, which would determine if this race condition is actually possible.

I'm tracing whether onMutate's await yields a microtask before or after React's synchronous flush at the end of the discrete event dispatch — since React 18 flushes discrete updates synchronously within `flushSyncWorkOnAllRoots` right as the handler returns, timing of that microtask relative to the flush call matters a lot for whether the state update lands in the same paint or gets deferred.

That means mutationFn ends up reading `this.options` after the re-render already swapped in the new selectedModel, since MutationObserver's setOptions calls `this.#currentMutation?.setOptions(this.options)` on every observer update, replacing the options the mutation was created with before mutationFn actually executes.

This confirms the race: the POST fires with claude-opus-5 instead of the model chosen when the user hit send, matching what the DB shows. Now I need to figure out the fix — the composer's selected model at send-time must be captured and threaded through so the created run always uses the model active when the message was sent, not whatever the observer swapped to during the re-render.

I'm sketching how to carry the composer's selection into the newly created run, treating it as an "acknowledged" selection tied to that run ID so later server hydration doesn't silently override it with a different version.

The real risk is that when the optimistic run finally hydrates with a server version, it could clobber the correct model choice if the dirty fields aren't tracked properly — so I want to both mark the relevant fields dirty and make sure the optimistic snapshot already carries the right model, since the dirty flags self-clear once the server value matches. Next I need to fix the mutation function's race condition by capturing the selection at mutate time rather than later.

I'm weighing where to compute that selection — maybe wrapping the mutate call in useHomeMutations so it snapshots the selection right before calling runCommand.mutate with it included in the variables, rather than trying to stash it on a ref. The explicit approach of passing the resolved selection through the mutate variables feels cleanest and most deterministic.

Still writing mutation logic...

Actually, I should update HomeApp's `handleComposerRunCommand` to call `startConversation` instead of calling mutate directly, and make sure `onMutate` also pulls from `payload.launchSelection` for consistency so the optimistic snapshot carries the right model — though I want to check this isn't over-engineering it. Now I'm considering the fallback behavior for `resolveRunComposerSelection` when preferred worker data is missing.

If the client doesn't send a model patch, the run keeps its existing model, which is correct — so a server-side safety net isn't really feasible since the server can't distinguish a legitimate model switch from an erroneous one. That confirms the fix belongs on the client side, and `create.ts` just uses whatever the client sends, so that's fine too. I'm now checking whether there's a separate reversion path affecting later messages, since the user specifically mentioned the issue appeared by their second message.

I traced through catalog refreshes, which explicitly avoid touching saved session models, and confirmed that composer selection changes mid-turn get properly patched into the send call. I also checked whether `initializeComposerSelection` is invoked anywhere besides its definition in HomeUiStateManager, and it doesn't seem to be. Now I'm looking at how composer drafts get persisted to localStorage keyed by run to see if that's involved.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:45:26.625Z

I notice the codebase pattern of extracting pure helpers and testing them separately, so I'm designing a fix that resolves the launch selection at mutate() time instead of inside mutationFn, by adding an exported pure function that takes the conversation mode and related args.

The second fix involves carrying that selection into the newly created run's draft state. I need to seed the created run's selection in HomeUiStateManager before setSelectedRunId is called, so that when selectRun runs, the conversation opens with the model the user actually launched it with rather than defaulting incorrectly.

I'm deciding which selection to store — the raw composer selection the user picked, not a resolved one — and considering whether to mark all fields dirty. Marking the worker field dirty is tricky: if the user chose "auto", the run's actual resolved worker type will differ from "auto" forever, so hydration would never overwrite it and the UI would incorrectly stay on "auto" indefinitely, which changes current behavior in a way I need to think through more carefully.

I also want to check whether "dirty" fields actually gate what gets sent on subsequent messages, so I'm going to look more closely at the mutation function that sends conversation messages to see if it always includes preferred worker/model regardless of dirtiness.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:45:33.356Z

I'm tracing how the send-message mutation builds its payload — in auto-selection mode it sends a null model but still passes the concrete worker type, so I'm checking how the server route translates that into the preference patch it applies.

If readStringPatch treats null as a "reset" instruction, that means every message sent while the composer is set to auto would wipe the run's saved model preference entirely, causing hydration to fall back to catalog[0] (Opus) afterward. This looks like a second, quite plausible explanation for the model silently changing after the first message, so I want to read through the actual route handler to confirm.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:45:46.016Z

This confirms that sending null for preferredWorkerModel when worker is "auto" actually wipes the conversation's stored model, causing the composer to fall back to catalog[0], which happens to be Opus 5 for Claude — exactly matching what the user described if their composer worker was set to "auto".

That confirms resetting to null means the next launch falls back to the worker's default rather than what actually ran before — silently changing the model. I also need to check whether the composer even shows a model picker when the worker is auto, since if it does but the selection gets nulled anyway, that's a second bug worth flagging. Let me look at how ConversationComposer handles model display for auto worker selection.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:46:00.910Z

I notice the model picker is shown even when worker is set to auto, so a user's selection gets silently dropped as null and the run's stored model resets — clearly the broken behavior the user is upset about. The fix needs to ensure the conversation always reuses the model from the previous message, so I'm identifying the client paths responsible for these silent swaps.

I'm capturing the launch selection at mutation time to close the create race, seeding the new run's composer draft with that selection so the picker doesn't flip after sending, and including model plus effort in the optimistic snapshot so hydration doesn't need to change anything. For auto-worker conversations, instead of sending null when a concrete worker type resolves and the user has picked a model from that worker's catalog, I should send the actual model — and when there's nothing to send, omit the key entirely rather than sending null, since an absent key means the server keeps the previous model rather than resetting it. That matches exactly what the user wants, though I want to be careful this doesn't introduce unintended scope creep into auto-worker behavior.

Since there's no "auto" option in the model picker itself — only concrete catalog entries — there's never a legitimate user intent to reset the model to null, so always omitting the reset when no explicit choice exists should be safe. I'll apply the same logic to the create path: only send null for `preferredWorkerModel` when it's genuinely an auto-worker selection with no visible model choice, otherwise send the resolved model.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:46:20.541Z

I've confirmed that for "auto", the model picker shows the auto-resolved worker's models, so the displayed model matters and sending null would discard it. Though the user's session is in direct mode where the worker is never "auto," this is still the same class of bug—silently changing the model—so I'll fix it anyway.

Rather than actively sending the auto-resolved model, I realize the safer minimal fix is to simply never send a model reset at all—omit the key entirely, which preserves whatever model was previously used without pinning anything the user didn't explicitly choose. This satisfies the invariant that the model should always carry over from the previous message.

But there's a risk I need to check: if `resolveSelectedWorkerModel` picks a model tied to a different worker type than the one actually resolved for auto mode, we could end up pinning a nonsensical model that doesn't belong to the active worker's catalog at all.

The safer fix is to only send the model if it exists in the target worker's catalog options, otherwise omit the key entirely so it falls back to reuse the previous value. I need to figure out if `useHomeMutations` already has access to that catalog, or whether I should just pass `activeWorkerModelOptions` in as a prop since that's already computed in the view model.

For the auto worker case, the target type at send time is `autoSelectedWorkerType`, which matches `activeWorkerModelType`, so they're equivalent here. Now I'll look at how `useHomeMutations`' props interface is defined and where it's constructed.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:46:43.007Z

I'm designing a new pure module for composer launch selection that resolves the worker type, auto-selection state, and model based on effort values and selected worker options.

I'm working through the model resolution rule, checking that the resolved model exists in the available options for the active worker type, and thinking through an edge case around gateway model namespacing.

Then I look at how the request body is built for worker preferences, omitting `preferredWorkerModel` when there's no selection so the server retains the previous model — which matches the requirement, though I want to confirm whether the runtime API types actually allow this field to be optional for both `create` and `sendTo` calls, so I need to check the conversations route signature.

Now I'm working out the changes needed for `runCommand`: the payload should carry a full `ComposerLaunchSelection` plus conversation mode, `mutationFn` should read exclusively from `payload.launch` instead of any render-closure state, and `onMutate` needs to seed the new run's composer draft and include model/effort in its optimistic snapshot. For `handleComposerRunCommand` in HomeApp, I'm thinking the cleanest approach is to expose a `startConversation` helper from `useHomeMutations` that wraps the mutate call and computes the launch selection from the hook's props at call time.

I'm considering whether to memoize the launch selection versus recomputing it inline via a callback, weighing dependency-array correctness against unnecessary re-renders, and leaning toward a `useMemo` that recalculates per render since that keeps things simple and correct.

For `sendConversationMessage`, I'm thinking through whether the same race condition applies—since its mutation doesn't mutate selection state, it likely doesn't need the same fix, but adding `launch` to its payload anyway would keep the API consistent and avoid the auto-null-reset issue, so I'm leaning toward doing it for uniformity even though it's not strictly required for safety.

Since `sendConversationMessage` has several call sites, I want to check where it's actually invoked before wrapping it.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:47:21.704Z

Good, all runtimes post JSON to the same routes so absence is preserved consistently. I'm starting a new file to define the composer launch selection logic, capturing the worker, model, effort, and account tied to a send action.

Reading these values from live composer state inside a mutation function is unsafe since React re-renders before the mutation runs and reads lazily, so I need to pass the selection through mutation variables to freeze the launch identity against that race. I should also include conversationMode since it's vulnerable to the same reset bug — if a user picks "omni" but the draft resets to "direct" before the mutation fires, that's the identical class of issue, so I'll freeze that value too.

Writing the type and resolver function...

Finishing the allowed worker types logic, then defining a function that builds the launch preference body from a composer selection, carefully omitting rather than nulling the preferred worker model field since the message routes treat an empty string differently from an absent one.

I'm now double-checking that `preferredWorkerType` matches between the create and send paths, and confirming that sending null intentionally triggers a reset operation that clears the field -- this is existing behavior I want to preserve rather than change.

I'll keep allowedWorkerTypes logic unchanged since it matches my version. The one real behavior change is that preferredWorkerModel now gets set to the picker's resolved model even in auto-worker mode, instead of null—meaning a new auto-worker conversation would now pin the displayed model, which I think is the more correct behavior but worth flagging as a deviation.

Now I need to work through the useHomeMutations changes: importing the new module, computing composerLaunchSelection, and adding an activeWorkerModelValues prop that gets threaded from HomeApp or the view model. I'm checking where useHomeMutations is actually called from to figure out where to wire this new prop in.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:48:18.863Z

That effect intentionally avoids replacing with catalog[0], keeping the user's selected model even if unavailable, and `composerModelOptions` reflects that unavailable state. I'm tracing whether the composer actually receives `composerModelOptions` or `activeWorkerModelOptions` as a prop, checking how HomeApp passes it down to ComposerContainer.

Now I'm weighing whether my `activeWorkerModelValues` guard should check against `composerModelOptions` — sending an unavailable model could break the launch, but adding a catalog check risks newly dropping a model the user deliberately set, since the current code sends `resolvedSelectedModel` unconditionally without any catalog validation. This could regress the create-conversation path if the catalog hasn't merged gateway models yet, so I'm reconsidering the original purpose of the guard, which was just to prevent pinning a stale cross-worker default.

I'm now leaning toward simplifying further: just always send `resolveSelectedWorkerModel(workerType, selectedModel)` when non-empty, since the picker's value is already synced to the active worker's options by the reset-on-worker-change logic. The only edge case is the initial default model showing as "unavailable" under an auto-resolved worker — but since the user can visibly see that state in the picker, sending it anyway is at least honest, even if not ideal.

Rather than threading through the catalog guard, I'll simplify: always send the resolved model when non-empty, whether from an explicit worker or auto, and never send null/reset. This does mean a stale default could get pinned under the wrong worker, but that's a pre-existing display issue the picker already surfaces as "unavailable," not something my change meaningfully worsens.

I can actually eliminate even that narrow risk cheaply — keep the catalog membership check only for the auto case, where today's behavior is already null, so falling back to "omit" is never worse. For explicit selection, always send the resolved value unchanged. That gives me a strictly no-regression rule for both paths, with `buildLaunchPreferenceBody` omitting the model key instead of sending null so it preserves rather than resets the previous value. Now I need to think through how the create path should handle omitting versus sending null.

For create, omitting and sending null are equivalent since it's a new run either way, so that path stays fine. For send with explicit worker, resolved is non-empty and behaves as today; for send with auto, this now correctly preserves instead of resetting, fixing that bug. I still need the list of allowed model values for the auto branch, so I'll pass it down from HomeApp — checking the hook ordering, `activeWorkerModelOptions` is destructured before `useHomeMutations` is called, so that's fine. Now I'm moving to the HomeUiStateManager piece that seeds a newly created conversation's draft with the composer's current selection.

Finishing writing the draft object...

But I'm second-guessing whether dirtySelectionFields is actually necessary here — if I instead fix the optimistic run snapshot to include the model and effort upfront, hydration would already match and the dirty-tracking wouldn't be needed. Though I need to check how `resolveRunComposerSelection` handles the worker field, since an auto selection could get overwritten by the optimistic run's concrete preferredWorkerType.

The trickier case is when model is null under auto selection: hydration falls back to catalog[0], which could silently swap out the model the user actually saw, and that's precisely the mismatch this is supposed to avoid — though it's unavoidable when the chosen model isn't even in the catalog.

So I'm leaning toward not marking any fields dirty, since seeding the optimistic run with model, effort, type, and accountId should make the resolved selection match what was seeded, aside from worker (auto resolving to concrete) and conversationMode being already handled. Though I need to double check how resolveComposerEffortLabel interprets whatever effort value I put on the optimistic run to make sure the label comes out right.

Actually seeding is necessary regardless of snapshot completeness — selectRun fires in onMutate and resets composer state immediately, while hydration only runs after render, so there'd still be a visible flash. I should seed before calling setSelectedRunId to avoid that gap.

The snapshot also needs to carry the model and effort — adding `preferredWorkerModel` and `preferredWorkerEffort` to the snapshot builder's args and output, confirming RunRecord already supports those fields. For tests, I'm planning unit coverage for the resolution rules (explicit vs auto worker model handling, omitting keys appropriately, never emitting a reset), plus extending the state manager and run-selection-effects tests to verify seeding preserves the model after selectRun.

I want to match the style of the existing home-ui-state-manager test before writing new ones, and I need to trace through useHomeMutations' create/send logic and confirm WorkerType is importable, checking that types.ts just re-exports. Now I'm ready to write the new module.

import { HomeUiStateManager } from "@/interface/home/HomeUiStateManager";

  "content": "import type { ComposerWorkerOption, ConversationModeOption, WorkerType } from \"./types\";\nimport { resolveComposerEffortValue, resolveSelectedWorkerModel } from \"./utils\";\n\n/**\n * The worker identity a send is launched with, frozen at the moment the user\n * hit send.\n *\n * Reading the live composer state from inside a mutation function is not safe.\n * `runCommand.onMutate` selects the conversation it is about to create, React\n * flushes that render before the awaited mutation function runs, and React\n * Query reads `options.mutationFn` lazily — so the request went out with\n * whatever the freshly selected (draftless) conversation had fallen back to,\n * which is the first model in the worker's catalogue. Carrying the selection\n * through the mutation variables makes the launch identity immune to any\n * re-render that happens between `mutate()` and the request.\n */\nexport type ComposerLaunchSelection = {\n  conversationMode: ConversationModeOption;\n  workerType: WorkerType | null;\n  isAutoWorkerSelection: boolean;\n  /** `null` means \"keep the model this conversation already runs on\". */\n  model: string | null;\n  effort: string;\n  accountId: string | null;\n  allowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];\n};\n\nexport function resolveComposerLaunchSelection(args: {\n  conversationMode: ConversationModeOption;\n  selectedCliAgent: ComposerWorkerOption;\n  selectedModel: string;\n  selectedEffort: string;\n  selectedWorkerAccountId: string;\n  autoSelectedWorkerType: WorkerType | null;\n  activeAllowedWorkerTypes: WorkerType[];\n  activeWorkerModelValues: string[];\n}): ComposerLaunchSelection {\n  const isAutoWorkerSelection = args.selectedCliAgent === \"auto\";\n  const workerType = isAutoWorkerSelection ? args.autoSelectedWorkerType : args.selectedCliAgent;\n  const resolvedModel = workerType\n    ? resolveSelectedWorkerModel(workerType, args.selectedModel).trim()\n    : \"\";\n  // An explicit worker owns the model picker, so its value is asserted as-is.\n  // Under \"auto\" the picker follows whichever worker the policy resolves to,\n  // and a model left over from a different worker is not a choice for this one\n  // — leaving it out keeps the conversation on the model it already ran.\n  const model = isAutoWorkerSelection\n    ? args.activeWorkerModelValues.includes(resolvedModel) ? resolvedModel : null\n    : resolvedModel || null;\n\n  return {\n    conversationMode: args.conversationMode,\n    workerType,\n    isAutoWorkerSelection,\n    model,\n    effort: resolveComposerEffortValue(args.selectedEffort),\n    accountId: args.selectedWorkerAccountId === \"auto\" ? null : args.selectedWorkerAccountId,\n    allowedWorkerTypes: isAutoWorkerSelection\n      ? args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes\n      : workerType\n        ? [workerType]\n        : args.activeAllowedWorkerTypes,\n  };\n}\n\n/**\n * The worker-preference fields of a create/send request body.\n *\n * `preferredWorkerModel` is omitted rather than sent as null when there is no\n * model to assert: the message route reads a present-but-empty value as \"reset\n * this conversation's model\", and that reset is what dropped a conversation\n * back onto the first model in the catalogue on its second message.\n */\nexport function buildLaunchPreferenceBody(selection: ComposerLaunchSelection) {\n  return {\n    preferredWorkerType: selection.workerType,\n    preferredWorkerEffort: selection.effort,\n    preferredWorkerAccountId: selection.accountId,\n    allowedWorkerTypes: selection.allowedWorkerTypes,\n    ...(selection.model ? { preferredWorkerModel: selection.model } : {}),\n  };\n}\n"
}

  "content": "import { describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\";\nimport {\n  buildLaunchPreferenceBody,\n  resolveComposerLaunchSelection,\n} from resolveComposerLaunchSelection>[0]> = {}) {\n  return resolveComposerLaunchSelection({\n    conversationMode: \"direct\",\n    selectedCliAgent: \"claude\",\n    selectedModel: \"claude-fable-5-1\",\n    selectedEffort: \"High\",\n    selectedWorkerAccountId: \"auto\",\n    autoSelectedWorkerType: \"claude\",\n    activeAllowedWorkerTypes: [\"claude\"],\n    activeWorkerModelValues: CLAUDE_MODELS,\n    ...overrides,\n  });\n}\n\ndescribe(\"composer launch selection\", () => {\n  it(\"carries the model the composer is showing\", () => {\n    expect(resolve()).toMatchObject({\n      workerType: \"claude\",\n      model: \"claude-fable-5-1\",\n      effort: \"high\",\n      accountId: null,\n      allowedWorkerTypes: [\"claude\"],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it(\"keeps the picked model when the worker is resolved automatically\", () => {\n    expect(resolve({\n      selectedCliAgent: \"auto\",\n      autoSelectedWorkerType: \"claude\",\n      activeAllowedWorkerTypes: [\"claude\", \"codex\"],\n    })).toMatchObject({\n      workerType: \"claude\",\n      isAutoWorkerSelection: true,\n      model: \"claude-fable-5-1\",\n      allowedWorkerTypes: [\"claude\", \"codex\"],\n    });\n  });\n\n  it(\"asserts no model when an automatic worker inherits another worker's leftover choice\", () => {\n    expect(resolve({\n      selectedCliAgent: \"auto\",\n      selectedModel: \"gpt-5.6-sol\",\n      autoSelectedWorkerType: \"claude\",\n    }).model).toBeNull();\n  });\n\n  it(\"never emits a model reset, so a conversation keeps the model its last message ran on\", () => {\n    const body = buildLaunchPreferenceBody(resolve({\n      selectedCliAgent: \"auto\",\n      selectedModel: \"gpt-5.6-sol\",\n      autoSelectedWorkerType: \"claude\",\n    }));\n\n    expect(body).not.toHaveProperty(\"preferredWorkerModel\");\n    expect(Object.keys(body)).toEqual([\n      \"preferredWorkerType\",\n      \"preferredWorkerEffort\",\n expect(buildLaunchPreferenceBody(resolve())).toMatchObject({\n      preferredWorkerType: \"claude\",\n      preferredWorkerModel: \"claude-fable-5-1\",\n      preferredWorkerEffort: \"high\",\n    });\n  });\n\n  it(\"passes an explicit account through and normalizes opencode model ids\", () => {\n    expect(resolve({\n      selectedCliAgent: \"opencode\",\n      selectedModel: ```text
      hydratedRunSelectionId: "run-a",
    });
  });

  it("clears a dirty selection that returns to the unchanged server value", () => {
    const manager = new HomeUiStateManager();
    const baseline = { conversationMode: "direct", worker: "codex", accountId: "auto", model: "model-a", effort: "High" } as const;
    manager.selectRun("run-a");
    manager.hydrateComposerSelection({ runId: "run-a", selection: baseline, serverVersion: "1" });
    manager.setComposerSelectionField("model", "model-b");
    manager.setComposerSelectionField("model", "model-a");

    expect(manager.getSnapshot().composerDraftsByRun["run-a"]?.dirtySelectionFields).not.toContain("model");
  });

  it("changes worker and its compatible model atomically", () => {
    const manager = new HomeUiStateManager();
    manager.setComposerSelectionField("worker", "codex");
    manager.setComposerSelectionField("model", "gpt-5.6-sol");
    let notifications = 0;
    manager.subscribe(() => {
      notifications += 1;
    });

    manager.setComposerWorkerSelection("claude", "claude-opus-5");

    expect(manager.getSnapshot()).toMatchObject({
      selectedCliAgent: "claude",
      selectedModel: "claude-opus-5",
    });
    expect(manager.getSnapshot().composerDraftsByRun.__new__?.selection).toMatchObject({
      worker: "claude",
      model: "claude-opus-5",
    });
    expect(manager.getSnapshot().composerDraftsByRun.__new__?.dirtySelectionFields).toEqual(
      expect.arrayContaining(["worker", "model"]),
    );
    expect(notifications).toBe(1);
  });
});

import { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { useHomeMutations } from "@/interface/home/useHomeMutations";
import { homeUiStateManager } from "@/interface/home/HomeUiStateManager";
import { createFetchRuntimeRequest } from "@/runtime-api/request";

// Expose the real mutation callbacks without mounting the entire home screen.
vi.mock("@tanstack/react-query", async (importOriginal) => ({
  ...await importOriginal<typeof import("@tanstack/react-query")>(),
  useMutation: (options: unknown) => options,
}));
vi.mock("@/runtime-api/provider", () => ({ useRuntimeAPIs: () => ({}) }));

describe("home authentication failures", () => {
  beforeEach(() => {
    homeUiStateManager.patch(() => ({ authError: null, pairRedeemError: null }));
  });

  it.each([
    ["loginMutation", "authError", 401, "Incorrect password."],
    ["loginMutation", "authError", 403, "Cross-site request rejected."],
    ["redeemPairMutation", "pairRedeemError", 400, "Pairing token expired."],
  ] as const)("preserves %s %s errors (HTTP %s)", async (mutation, field, status, message) => {
    const request = createFetchRuntimeRequest({
      surface: "web",
      fetchImpl: async () => Response.json({ error: { message } }, { status }),
    });
    const error = await request("POST", "/api/auth/login").catch((failure: unknown) => failure);
    const mutations = useHomeMutations({} as Parameters<typeof useHomeMutations>[0]);
    const callback = mutations[mutation] as unknown as { onError(error: unknown): void };
    callback.onError(error);
    expect(homeUiStateManager.getSnapshot()[field]).toBe(message);
  });
});
