## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:52:39.181Z

Since the test calls the hook outside React with empty params and only mocks useMutation, not React hooks themselves, I need another approach. Rather than mocking useMemo/useCallback in the test, it's simpler to avoid React hooks in useHomeMutations entirely — compute composerLaunchSelection as a plain const and startConversation as a plain function, since the values are cheap enough not to need memoization.

Actually, thinking through the perf implications more carefully: if startConversation's identity changes every render, that would ripple into HomeApp's useCallback dependencies and cause unnecessary re-renders in the composer children. The current useMemo with primitive deps keeps composerLaunchSelection's identity stable, and useCallback keeps startConversation stable — that's actually the right call for performance, so I should keep the hooks as-is and instead fix the test itself, since passing `{}` as params should just destructure to undefined values without crashing.

The real issue is that these hooks are being called outside a React component. The cleanest fix is mocking react's useMemo/useCallback in the test so they just invoke the factory/callback directly, preserving the test's intent of exercising the real mutation logic without needing a full component mount.

Let me check the other three failures before settling on this approach.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:56:32.679Z

I've confirmed those supervisor failures pre-exist at HEAD and aren't related to my change, and the mac-install failure just needs a build. I'm cleaning up the worktree now, and going back to verify the remaining reversion vector — checking that my fix covers the create-time race, the composer flip after create, and auto-workflow behavior.

No lingering mid-conversation model reset exists since server-driven changes to `run.preferredWorkerModel` are the correct authoritative path. I'm also tracing through `adoptSelectionForCreatedRun` and `selectRun` to confirm the draft selection and hydration state end up correct once the run hydrates, and that matches the prior behavior where `hydratedRunSelectionId` starts null.

Verifying that once the optimistic run's fields resolve — model, worker type, effort, account, mode — they all match what the server eventually returns, so hydration becomes a no-op when the real run arrives, since `preferredWorkerRevision` defaults to 0 in both cases and other fields line up identically.

That discrepancy would just briefly diverge before self-correcting on the real run, which is acceptable. Checking allowedWorkerTypes in the send body, my version handles the null-workerType case slightly better than before by falling back to activeAllowedWorkerTypes. I also confirm ComposerDraftPersistence will correctly pick up the new run key after adoptSelectionForCreatedRun since it persists whatever is in localStorage.

I don't think adoptSelectionForCreatedRun needs to handle sends from within an existing conversation, since runCommand only ever creates new conversations. What I really want is a focused test verifying that runCommand.mutationFn uses payload.launch rather than live state — that's the core regression guard, so I'll write it in the style of home-auth-errors.test.ts, mocking useMutation, calling useHomeMutations, invoking mutationFn with a stub runtime API, and calling onMutate first with a different live selection to simulate the reset and assert the request body stays correct.

For the mocks I need useMutation, useMemo/useCallback, and useRuntimeAPIs (with a spy on conversations.create and a files stub). I also want to check how uploadPendingChatAttachments handles an empty attachments array — it's a short file, so I should check whether it short-circuits before touching the files API.

```text
"use client";

import type { ChatAttachment, PendingChatAttachment } from "@/lib/chat-attachments";
import type { RuntimeAPIs } from "@/runtime-api/types";

/**
 * Upload pending composer attachments and return their persisted descriptors.
 * Shared by the conversation-send and queued-message mutation hooks.
 */
export async function uploadPendingChatAttachments(
  attachments: PendingChatAttachment[],
  filesApi: Pick<RuntimeAPIs["files"], "upload">,
): Promise<ChatAttachment[]> {
  if (attachments.length === 0) {
    return [];
  }

  const formData = new FormData();
  attachments.forEach((attachment) => formData.append("files", attachment.file, attachment.name));
  const response = await filesApi.upload(formData) as {
    ok: true;
    attachments: ChatAttachment[];
  };

  return response.attachments;
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:57:06.354Z

Right — the singleton `homeUiStateManager` holds the actual composer state, which this test never set to claude/fable since the hook props are separate from the singleton. `adoptSelectionForCreatedRun` copies from that singleton rather than from props, and in the real app they agree because props derive from the singleton via useHomeViewModel — but in the test they diverge, and I'm also noticing the first test left leaked state behind.

I'm weighing whether to make `adoptSelectionForCreatedRun` accept the launch selection explicitly instead of reading the singleton, but realize reading the live singleton is actually correct behavior for the display draft — it just means the test setup needs to keep the singleton in sync. I'll fix this by setting the singleton's selection to claude/fable in beforeEach and clean up a stray leftover variable in the test.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:57:20.362Z

There's still leakage between tests: test 1 leaves the `run-new` draft set to model claude-opus-5, so `adoptSelectionForCreatedRun` in test 3 becomes a no-op since that draft already exists. I also need to check which two tests are actually failing now — likely the "hands the launch selection" and "gives the created conversation a placeholder" cases.

I'm planning to add a proper reset in beforeEach that clears composerDraftsByRun and resets the selected run id, then reapplies the worker selection so the fresh draft gets created correctly. I'm also considering using unique run ids per test as a cleaner alternative to avoid this state-reset trickery altogether.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:57:34.528Z

All four tests pass, so now I want to make sure they'd actually catch a regression by mentally reverting the key behavior — specifically checking that if mutationFn read live state instead of the captured payload, the first test would correctly fail since it mutates state after onMutate and expects the original snapshot value to persist.

Actually, I realize the test as written wouldn't catch the real race, since props are static in this construction and the old buggy code would also pass by coincidence. The actual race happens when React re-renders with new props between onMutate and mutationFn, so I need to restructure the test to build mutations twice — once for the initial render, then again to simulate a re-render with different state, and call the second render's mutationFn using the first render's variables — which properly fails under the old implementation. I'll parameterize buildMutations with overrides to support this.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T20:58:28.661Z

I'm hitting typecheck errors because the mocked useMutation return doesn't match the UseMutationResult type, so I need to cast it like the existing home-auth-errors test does. I'm defining a helper type for the mutation internals so I can safely cast onMutate and mutationFn.

Also fixing the mock call array typing by giving the vi.fn a typed parameter instead of leaving it inferred as an empty tuple, then rewriting the test file cleanly. I checked the useHomeViewModel eslint warning about the unused disable comment and confirmed it's pre-existing since I never touched that file.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-19T21:01:23.675Z

That path is unaffected too since it just calls setSelectedModel. I'm checking the vscode and mobile surfaces to confirm they share the same interface code and don't have separate calls creating conversations with preferences.

Earlier grep confirmed client-side create calls only exist in useHomeMutations, so I'm re-running the test suites once more to confirm everything passes. Now I'm considering whether to record a memory about this — the user has been burned by this bug repeatedly, and the underlying invariant ("always reuse the previous message's model, never send a model reset") isn't obvious from the code alone, so it seems worth capturing as a project-level memory even though the fix itself is now encoded in tests and comments.

I'll save a concise project memory pointing to this invariant. I also want to flag a subtle behavior change in my summary: with worker="auto" in omni mode, the create request now pins the displayed model when the previous code sent null, since the auto-resolved worker's model is typically included in activeWorkerModelValues — this is a deliberate change to match what the picker actually shows, and I should call it out explicitly before wrapping up.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/masterman/.claude/projects/-Users-masterman-NLP-omniharness/memory/conversation-model-must-never-change-itself.md",
  "content": "---\nname: conversation-model-must-never-change-itself\ndescription: OmniHarness rule — a conversation always reuses the model its previous message ran on; nothing may swap it silently\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nHard product invariant for OmniHarness (stated emphatically on 2026-09-19, after repeated recurrences): a conversation must **always** reuse the model the previous message in that conversation ran on. The composer must never silently swap a user's model pick, and a request must never reset a conversation's stored model.\n\n**Why:** the model picker reverting to the first catalogue entry (Opus 5 for Claude) has burned the user repeatedly across several attempted fixes. Two mechanisms caused it, both client-side: (1) React Query reads `options.mutationFn` lazily, so `runCommand.onMutate` selecting the new conversation re-rendered the composer to its defaults *before* the create request was built; (2) `preferredWorkerModel: null` in a send body is read by the routes as \"reset this conversation's model\".\n\n**How to apply:** build create/send worker preferences from a selection frozen at `mutate()` time (`src/interface/home/composer-launch-selection.ts`), never from live composer state inside a mutation function. Omit `preferredWorkerModel` rather than sending null/empty. Any new send path must do the same. Related: [[text-must-wrap-inside-its-container]].\n"
}

uuid: 75124a3a-23b3-476a-a0a3-f884886738db
parent: eb0fdfe1-1c0a-4c93-9016-062e0c6ad3ac
```

## Claude · 2026-09-19T21:01:45.667Z

Found and fixed it. Your session `be435712032c` is proof: the run row has `preferred_worker_model = claude-opus-5` with `preferred_worker_revision = 0` — meaning Opus 5 wasn't swapped in later, it went out on the **create** request. The composer said Fable 5.1; the request said Opus 5.

**Two separate defects, both client-side:**

1. **The create request was built after the composer had already reset itself.** `runCommand.onMutate` selects the brand-new conversation (`src/interface/home/useHomeMutations.ts:534`). That run has no composer draft yet, so `selectRun` fell back to `EMPTY_COMPOSER_DRAFT`, then hydrated from an optimistic run record that carried *no model* — and `resolveRunComposerSelection` fills a missing model with `catalog[0]`, which for Claude is `claude-opus-5`. React flushes that render before the awaited `mutationFn` runs, and React Query reads `options.mutationFn` lazily from the *newest* render (`query-core/mutation.js:74`, after the `await onMutate` at line 102). So the request closed over the reset selection. That is the exact Fable → Opus 5 flip.

2. **Sending `preferredWorkerModel: null` wipes the conversation's model.** `conversation-messages.ts:71` reads a present-but-empty value as `{operation: "reset"}`. Every message sent with the worker on "auto" reset the run's stored model to null, after which the composer hydrated to `catalog[0]` again. Second message, Opus 5.

**What changed**

- New `src/interface/home/composer-launch-selection.ts` — the worker/model/effort/account are frozen at `mutate()` time and travel through the mutation variables. Nothing downstream re-reads live composer state.
- `buildLaunchPreferenceBody` **omits** `preferredWorkerModel` instead of sending null, so the server keeps the model the previous message ran on. A reset is now unreachable from the UI.
- `HomeUiStateManager.adoptSelectionForCreatedRun` hands the launch selection to the conversation it created, so the picker doesn't flip after the first message.
- The optimistic run placeholder now carries the model and effort, so hydration converges instead of falling through to `catalog[0]`.
- All four request builders (create, send, preflight answer, project commit) go through the same frozen selection.

**One deliberate behavior change:** with the worker on "auto", the model shown in the picker is now sent rather than dropped — previously your pick was silently discarded and the server chose. It's only sent when it belongs to the resolved worker's catalogue.

**Verification:** full suite 3425 passed / 504 files; the one failure (`mac-install-workflow`) is a missing `.github/workflows/install-smoke.yml`, pre-existing and unrelated. `npm run typecheck` and eslint clean. I added `tests/app/composer-launch-request.test.ts`, which reproduces the re-render race; I confirmed 3 of its 4 tests fail against the old code and pass against the new.
